\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {M} _ {k + 1} ^ {\tilde {w}} \leq \widetilde {G} _ {0: k} ^ {(2)} \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {w}} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {w}} \gamma_ {k + 1} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {3} ^ {\tilde {w}} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} \mathrm {M} _ {k + 1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {2} ^ {\tilde {w}} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} ^ {2} \widetilde {G} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(2)} \Bigl \{\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \prod_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} \Bigl (1 - \beta_ {i} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{8} \Bigr) + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{4} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 8} \beta_ {j} \Bigr \} \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \widetilde {G} _ {0: k} ^ {(2)} \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {w}} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {w}} \gamma_ {k + 1} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {3} ^ {\tilde {w}} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} \mathrm {M} _ {k + 1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {2} ^ {\tilde {w}} \gamma_ {k + 1} \Bigl \{\frac {\mathrm {C} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 8}}{1 - \beta_ {0} a _ {\Delta} / 8} \prod_ {i = 0} ^ {k} \Bigl (1 - \beta_ {i} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{8} \Bigr) + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{4} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 8} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 4} \Bigr \} \\ \leq \left\{\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {w}} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {2} ^ {\tilde {w}} \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{4} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 8} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 4} \right\} \gamma_ {k + 1} + \left\{\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {w}} + \frac {\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {2} ^ {\tilde {w}} \mathrm {C} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 8}}{1 - \beta_ {0} a _ {\Delta} / 8} \right\} \prod_ {i = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \beta_ {i} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{8}\right) + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {3} ^ {\tilde {w}} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} \mathrm {M} _ {k + 1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used  $\widetilde{G}_{j + 1:k}^{(2)}\leq \left(\prod_{i = j + 1}^{k}(1 - \gamma_{i}a_{22} / 8)\right)^{2}$  in (a). As such, together with (79) this gives

$$
\boxed {\mathrm {M} _ {k + 1} ^ {\widehat {w}} \leq \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\widehat {w}} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \beta_ {\ell} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{8}\right) + \mathrm {C} _ {1} ^ {\widehat {w}, \mathsf {m a r k}} \gamma_ {k + 1},}
$$

where

$$
\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {w}} := 2 \left\{\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {w}} + \frac {\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {2} ^ {\tilde {w}} \mathrm {C} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 8}}{1 - \beta_ {0} a _ {\Delta} / 8} + (1 + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {3} ^ {\tilde {w}}) \mathrm {L} _ {\infty} ^ {2} \frac {\lambda_ {\max } (Q _ {2 2})}{\lambda_ {\min } (Q _ {\Delta})} \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {0} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \right\} \tag {80}
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {1} ^ {\widehat {w}, \mathsf {m a r k}} := 2 \left\{\widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {w}} + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {2} ^ {\tilde {w}} \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{4} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 8} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 4} + \kappa (1 + \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {3} ^ {\tilde {w}}) \mathrm {L} _ {\infty} ^ {2} \frac {\lambda_ {\max} (Q _ {2 2})}{\lambda_ {\min} (Q _ {\Delta})} \widetilde {\mathrm {C}} _ {1} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{4} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 8} \right\}
$$

Similarly, as  $\widetilde{\mathrm{C}}_0^{\widehat{w}}\leq \mathrm{C}_0^{\widehat{w},\mathrm{mark}}(1 + \mathrm{V}_0)$  for some constant  $\mathrm{C}_0^{\widehat{w},\mathrm{mark}}$ , the above yields (15). We conclude the proof of Theorem 2.

# B.2.1. AUXILIARY RESULTS FOR THE MARKOVIAN NOISE CASE

Lemma 11 Let  $(a_{j})_{j\geq 0}$  be a sequence of  $d_{\theta}$ -dimensional vectors. The following equality holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} (a _ {j} - a _ {j + 1}) \\ = \beta_ {0} \Gamma_ {1: k} ^ {(1)} a _ {0} - \beta_ {k} a _ {k + 1} + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k} \left(\beta_ {j} ^ {2} B _ {1 1} ^ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} + (\beta_ {j} - \beta_ {j - 1}) \Gamma_ {j: k} ^ {(1)}\right) a _ {j} \tag {81} \\ \end{array}
$$

Similarly, for  $(b_j)_{j\geq 0}$  being a sequence of  $d_w$ -dimensional vectors, it holds:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(2)} (b _ {j} - b _ {j + 1}) \\ = \gamma_ {0} \Gamma_ {1: k} ^ {(2)} b _ {0} - \gamma_ {k} b _ {k + 1} + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k} \left(\gamma_ {j} ^ {2} B _ {2 2} ^ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(2)} + (\gamma_ {j} - \gamma_ {j - 1}) \Gamma_ {j: k} ^ {(1)}\right) b _ {j}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof We only prove (81). Observe the following chain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} (a _ {j} - a _ {j + 1}) = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} a _ {j} - \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} a _ {j + 1} \\ = \beta_ {0} \Gamma_ {1: k} ^ {(1)} a _ {0} - \beta_ {k} a _ {k + 1} + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k} \left(\beta_ {j} \Gamma_ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} - \beta_ {j - 1} \Gamma_ {j: k} ^ {(1)}\right) a _ {j} \\ \end{array}
$$

Using  $\beta_{j}\Gamma_{j + 1:k}^{(1)} - \beta_{j - 1}\Gamma_{j:k}^{(1)} = \beta_{j}^{2}B_{11}^{j}\Gamma_{j + 1:k}^{(1)} + (\beta_{j} - \beta_{j - 1})\Gamma_{j:k}^{(1)}$  concludes the proof.

Derivation of Eq. (63) The decomposition is obtained through repeatedly adding/subtracting terms. Particularly, we observe that the individual terms can be expressed as:

$$
C _ {j} \left(\widetilde {\psi} _ {j} ^ {b _ {1}} - \widetilde {\psi} _ {j + 1} ^ {b _ {1}}\right) = C _ {j} \widetilde {\psi} _ {j} ^ {b _ {1}} - C _ {j - 1} \widetilde {\psi} _ {j} ^ {b _ {1}} + C _ {j - 1} \widetilde {\psi} _ {j} ^ {b _ {1}} - C _ {j} \widetilde {\psi} _ {j + 1} ^ {b _ {1}}
$$

$$
\left(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 2}}\right) \tilde {w} _ {j} = \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j + 1} + \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 2}} \left(\tilde {w} _ {j + 1} - \tilde {w} _ {j}\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} C _ {j} \big (\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}} \big) \tilde {w} _ {j} = (C _ {j} - C _ {j - 1}) \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j} + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} (\tilde {w} _ {j} - \tilde {w} _ {j - 1}) \\ + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j - 1} - C _ {j} \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\left(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 1}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 1}}\right) \tilde {\theta} _ {j} = \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j + 1} + \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 1}} \left(\tilde {\theta} _ {j + 1} - \tilde {\theta} _ {j}\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} C _ {j} \big (\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \big) \tilde {\theta} _ {j} = (C _ {j} - C _ {j - 1}) \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} (\tilde {\theta} _ {j} - \tilde {\theta} _ {j - 1}) \\ + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j - 1} - C _ {j} \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 2}}\right) C _ {j - 1} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} = \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 2}} \left(C _ {j - 1} - C _ {j - 2}\right) \tilde {\theta} _ {j} + \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 2}} C _ {j - 2} \left(\tilde {\theta} _ {j} - \tilde {\theta} _ {j - 1}\right) \\ + \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {2 2}} C _ {j - 2} \tilde {\theta} _ {j - 1} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {2 2}} C _ {j - 1} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} C _ {j} (\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}}) C _ {j - 1} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} = (C _ {j} - C _ {j - 1}) \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} C _ {j - 1} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} (C _ {j - 1} - C _ {j - 2}) \tilde {\theta} _ {j} \\ + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} C _ {j - 2} \left(\tilde {\theta} _ {j} - \tilde {\theta} _ {j - 1}\right) \\ + C _ {j - 1} \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} C _ {j - 2} \tilde {\theta} _ {j - 1} - C _ {j} \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}} C _ {j - 1} \tilde {\theta} _ {j}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Collecting terms on the right hand side of the above equations yields (63). Moreover, we the vectors/matrices that appear in (63) can be bounded as

$$
\| \psi_ {j} ^ {W V} \| \leq \overline {{\mathsf {b}}} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}), \| \Upsilon_ {j} ^ {W V, \tilde {\theta}} \| \leq \overline {{\mathsf {A}}} (1 + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2}), \| \Upsilon_ {j} ^ {W V, \tilde {w}} \| \leq \overline {{\mathsf {A}}} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty})
$$

$$
\| \Phi^ {W V, \tilde {\theta}} \| \leq \overline {{\mathsf {A}}} (1 + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2}), \| \Phi^ {W V, \tilde {w}} \| \leq \overline {{\mathsf {A}}} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty})
$$

$$
\| \widetilde {\Psi} _ {j} ^ {W V, \tilde {\theta}} \| \leq \overline {{\mathrm {A}}} C _ {2} ^ {U} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}) (1 + \varsigma) \gamma_ {j}, \| \widetilde {\Psi} _ {j} ^ {W V, \tilde {w}} \| \leq \overline {{\mathrm {A}}} C _ {2} ^ {U} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2} \gamma_ {j}.
$$

where the last inequality is due to Lemma 20 and we have used  $\gamma_{j-1} \leq \varsigma \gamma_j$  [cf. A2-1]. Consequently, we can establish the bounds on the matrix/vector norms by setting

$$
\mathrm {E} _ {0} ^ {W V} := \max \{\overline {{\mathrm {b}}} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}), \overline {{\mathrm {A}}} (1 + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2}), \overline {{\mathrm {A}}} C _ {2} ^ {U} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}) (1 + \varsigma) \}.
$$

Derivation of Eq. (78) Setting  $\widetilde{\psi}_j^{b_1} := \psi_j^{b_1} + \Psi_j^{A_{11}}\theta^\star + \Psi_j^{A_{12}}w^\star$ , we observe

$$
\begin{array}{l} V _ {j + 1} ^ {(1)} = \left(\psi_ {j} ^ {b _ {1}} - \psi_ {j + 1} ^ {b _ {1}}\right) + \left(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}}\right) \theta_ {j} + \left(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}}\right) w _ {j} \\ = \widetilde {\psi} _ {j} ^ {b _ {1}} - \widetilde {\psi} _ {j + 1} ^ {b _ {1}} + (\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}}) \widetilde {\theta} _ {j} + (\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}}) \widetilde {w} _ {j} \\ \end{array}
$$

Similar to the previous paragraph, the decomposition is obtained through repeatedly adding/subtracting terms. We observe

$$
\left(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}}\right) \tilde {\theta} _ {j} = \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \tilde {\theta} _ {j + 1} + \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 1}} \left(\tilde {\theta} _ {j + 1} - \tilde {\theta} _ {j}\right)
$$

$$
{(\Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}}) \tilde {w} _ {j}} {= \Psi_ {j} ^ {A _ {1 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j} - \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}} \tilde {w} _ {j + 1} + \Psi_ {j + 1} ^ {A _ {1 2}} (\tilde {w} _ {j + 1} - \tilde {w} _ {j})}
$$

# Appendix C. Detailed Proof of Theorem 10

Throughout this section we will use additional notations. We denote

$$
\kappa_ {\ell} := \frac {\beta_ {\ell}}{\gamma_ {\ell}}.
$$

Let

$$
\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} := \kappa_ {\infty} \wedge (1 / 2) \left(\left\| A _ {1 2} \right\| C _ {\infty} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}}\right) ^ {- 1} \tag {82}
$$

and

$$
\beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} := \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} \wedge 1 / (4 \| \Delta \|) \tag {83}
$$

We assume  $\beta_{k} \leq \beta_{\infty}^{\exp}, \gamma_{k} \leq \gamma_{\infty}^{\mathrm{mtg}}, \kappa_{k} \leq \kappa \leq \kappa_{\infty}^{\exp}$ . Furthermore, let us define

$$
\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {m, n} ^ {(1)} := \prod_ {j = m} ^ {n} (\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {j} \Delta), \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {m, n} ^ {(2)} := \prod_ {j = m} ^ {n} (\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {j} A _ {2 2}).
$$

Using standard arguments we may bound operator norm of these matrices

$$
\left\| \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {m: n} ^ {(1)} \right\| \leq \sqrt {p _ {\Delta}} \prod_ {j = m} ^ {n} \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {j}\right), \left\| \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {m: n} ^ {(2)} \right\| \leq \sqrt {p _ {2 2}} \prod_ {j = m} ^ {n} \left(1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {j}\right) \tag {84}
$$

We set quantities  $m_W, m_V$  from the assumption 4 to be equal to

$$
m _ {W} := m _ {V} := \max (m _ {V W} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}, \| \Sigma^ {1 1} \|, \| \Sigma^ {1 2} \|, \| \Sigma^ {2 2} \|)
$$

All conditions of Theorem 1 are satisfied. We will use this theorem in the following form

$$
\mathrm {M} _ {k} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k}, \tag {85}
$$

$$
\mathrm {M} _ {k} ^ {\tilde {w}} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k - 1} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {k}, \tag {86}
$$

where  $\mathrm{C}_{0,\theta}^{\exp},\mathrm{C}_{1,\theta}^{\exp},\mathrm{C}_{0,w}^{\exp}$  and  $\mathrm{C}_{1,w}^{\exp}$  denote corresponding constants from Theorem 1. Similarly to (34) and (35) we can define  $\widetilde{m}_{VW}^{\exp}$ . Hence, the following inequality holds

$$
\| \mathbb {E} \left[ V _ {j} V _ {j} ^ {T} \right] \| \vee \| \mathbb {E} \left[ W _ {j} W _ {j} ^ {T} \right] \| \vee \| \mathbb {E} \left[ V _ {j} W _ {j} ^ {T} \right] \| \leq \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \left(1 + \mathrm {M} _ {k} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {k} ^ {\tilde {w}}\right)
$$

Applying (18) and (19) (compare with Konda and Tsitsiklis (2004)[Formula 4.4]) we may write down the following expansion for  $\tilde{\theta}_{k + 1}$ :

$$
\tilde {\theta} _ {k + 1} = S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} + \ldots + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)},
$$

where

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} := \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: k} ^ {(1)} \tilde {\theta} _ {0};
$$

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)} := \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: j} ^ {(2)} \tilde {w} _ {0};
$$

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} := \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \delta_ {j} ^ {(1)};
$$

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} := \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} (V _ {j + 1} + A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} W _ {j + 1});
$$

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)} := \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \Big (\sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {j - 1} \beta_ {\ell} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} \delta_ {\ell} ^ {(2)} \Big);
$$

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} := \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \Bigg (\sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {j - 1} \beta_ {\ell} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j} ^ {(2)} C _ {\ell} V _ {\ell + 1} \Bigg);
$$

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)} := \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {j - 1} \gamma_ {\ell} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} W _ {\ell + 1} - \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} W _ {\ell + 1},
$$

where

$$
\delta_ {\ell} ^ {(1)} := A _ {1 2} L _ {\ell} \tilde {\theta} _ {\ell}, \delta_ {\ell} ^ {(2)} := - C _ {k} A _ {1 2} \tilde {w} _ {\ell}.
$$

We will group all terms in the expansion into 5 blocks,  $S_{k+1}^{(0)} + S_{k+1}^{(1)}, S_{k+1}^{(2)} + S_{k+1}^{(5)}, S_{k+1}^{(3)}, S_{k+1}^{(4)}$  and  $S_{k+1}^{(6)}$ . It is easy to see that  $S_{k+1}^{(0)} + S_{k+1}^{(1)}$  is uncorrelated with  $S_{k+1}^{(3)}, S_{k+1}^{(6)}$  (moreover it is uncorrelated

with  $S_{k + 1}^{(5)}$ , but we ignore this fact). Since  $\mathbb{E}[\|\tilde{\theta}_{k + 1}\|^2] = \mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Tr}(\tilde{\theta}_{k + 1}\tilde{\theta}_{k + 1}^\top)]$  and by the linearity of trace using expansion we show

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \| \tilde {\theta} _ {k + 1} \| ^ {2} ] = \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathrm {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)}) ^ {\top}) \right] + J _ {k + 1} ^ {\prime},
$$

where for  $J_{k + 1}^{\prime}$  we will use the following crude estimate

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| J _ {k + 1} ^ {\prime} \right| \leq 3 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(\left\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)} \right\} \left\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)} \right\} ^ {\top}\right) \right] \\ + 5 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(\left\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \right\} \left\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \right\} ^ {\top}\right) + 5 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}\right) ^ {\top}\right) \right] \right. \\ + 2 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}) ^ {\top}) \right] + 2 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} \{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \} ^ {\top}) \right] \\ + 5 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}\right) ^ {\top}\right) \right] + 2 \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}\right) ^ {\top}\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Using martingale property and definition of  $\Sigma$  we rewrite the term  $\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Tr}(S_{k + 1}^{(3)}(S_{k + 1}^{(3)})^\top)]$  as follows

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right) = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \operatorname {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \Sigma \left[ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \right] ^ {\top}\right) \tag {87} \\ + \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \operatorname {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \left(\Sigma_ {j} - \Sigma\right) \left[ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \right] ^ {\top}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
\Sigma_ {j} := \mathbb {E} [ V _ {j} V _ {j} ^ {\top} ] + A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ W _ {j} W _ {j} ^ {\top} ] A _ {2 2} ^ {- \top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} + \mathbb {E} [ V _ {j} W _ {j} ^ {\top} ] A _ {2 2} ^ {- \top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} + A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ W _ {j} V _ {j} ^ {\top} ]
$$

Leading term in (87) For lower bound of the first term in (87) we will use the following fact. Since for any  $s \in [j + 1, k]$

$$
\left(\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {s} \Delta\right) ^ {\top} \left(\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {s} \Delta\right) = \mathrm {I} - \beta_ {s} \left(\Delta + \Delta^ {\top}\right) + \beta_ {s} ^ {2} \Delta^ {\top} \Delta \succeq \left(1 - 2 \beta_ {s} \| \Delta \|\right) \mathrm {I},
$$

we obtain using Lemma 12 (and remark after this lemma)

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \operatorname {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \Sigma \left[ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \right] ^ {\top}\right) \geq \beta_ {k + 1} \operatorname {T r} \Sigma \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} \left(1 - 2 \beta_ {\ell} \| \Delta\|\right) \geq C _ {3} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \operatorname {T r} \Sigma \tag {88}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3} ^ {\exp} := 1 / (8 \| \Delta \|) \tag {89}
$$

and we used  $\beta_{\infty}^{\mathrm{exp}} \leq 1 / (4\|\Delta\|)$  and  $k \geq k_0^{\mathrm{exp}}$ . To obtain upper bound we apply von Neumann trace inequality (i.e.  $\operatorname{Tr}(AB) \leq \sum_{j=1}^{n} a_j b_j$ , where  $\{a_j\}$  and  $\{b_j\}$  are non-increasing sequences of eigenvalues of Hermitian matrices  $A$  and  $B$  resp.) and Lemma 14

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \operatorname {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \Sigma \left[ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} \right] ^ {\top}\right) \leq p _ {\Delta} \operatorname {T r} (\Sigma) \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) ^ {2} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {4} ^ {\exp} \operatorname {T r} (\Sigma) \beta_ {k + 1} \tag {90}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {4} ^ {\exp} := p _ {\Delta} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} \tag {91}
$$

Inequalities (88) and (90) together imply (32).

Remainder term in (87) The second term in (87), which we denote by  $R_{k + 1}$  may be estimated as follows

$$
| R _ {k + 1} | \leq p _ {\Delta} d _ {\theta} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) ^ {2} (\mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {w}})
$$

Applying (85) and (86) and Lemma 14

$$
\boxed {| R _ {k + 1} | \leq C _ {3, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) V _ {0} \beta_ {k + 1} + C _ {3, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \gamma_ {k + 1},} \tag {92}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3, 0} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := p _ {\Delta} d _ {\theta} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} (\mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}) / (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}),
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := p _ {\Delta} d _ {\theta} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} (\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}})
$$

Estimation of  $J_{k+1}'$  To finish the proof of the theorem it remains to estimate  $J_{k+1}'$ . Applying (84) it is easy to check that

$$
\mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)}) ^ {\top} ]) = \mathrm {T r} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: k} ^ {(1)} \mathbb {E} [ \tilde {\theta} _ {0} \tilde {\theta} _ {0} ^ {\top} ] [ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: k} ^ {(1)} ] ^ {\top}) \leq p _ {\Delta} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| \tilde {\theta} _ {0} \| ^ {2} ]
$$

Similarly, recalling that  $\kappa_{\infty}^{\exp} \leq (1/4)a_{22}/a_{\Delta}$  and using  $\prod_{s=0}^{j}(1-a_{22}\gamma_s)(1-a_{\Delta}\beta_s)^{-1} \leq \prod_{s=0}^{j}(1-(a_{22}/2)\gamma_s)$  we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)}) ^ {\top} ]) = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \beta_ {\ell} \mathrm {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: j} ^ {(2)} \mathbb {E} [ \tilde {w} _ {0} \tilde {w} _ {0} ^ {\top} ] [ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: \ell} ^ {(2)} ] ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} [ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(1)} ] ^ {\top}\right) \\ \leq \mathrm {C} _ {1} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| \tilde {w} _ {0} \| ^ {2} ], \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := (4 / a _ {2 2} ^ {2}) p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} \| ^ {2} (\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}) ^ {2}
$$

Hence, we may conclude from the previous two inequalities that

$$
\boxed {\mathbb {E} [ \mathrm {T r} (\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)} \} \{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(0)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(1)} \} ^ {\top}) ] \leq C _ {0 + 1} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) ^ {2} \mathrm {V} _ {0}}, \tag {93}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {0 + 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := 2 p _ {\Delta} + 4 \mathrm {C} _ {1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2})
$$

For the next term in the expansion we have

$$
\mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} ]) \leq \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \beta_ {\ell} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \mathrm {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} L _ {j} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} \tilde {\theta} _ {l} ^ {\top} L _ {l} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {T} [ \Gamma_ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(1)} ] ^ {\top}\right) \right| \right]
$$

We apply Cauchy-Schwartz inequality twice, first  $|\operatorname{Tr}(AB^{\top})| \leq \operatorname{Tr}^{1/2}(AA^{\top})\operatorname{Tr}^{1/2}(BB^{\top})$  and then for expectation. We obtain

$$
\mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} ]) \leq \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \big (\mathrm {T r} \big (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} L _ {j} \mathbb {E} [ \tilde {\theta} _ {j} \tilde {\theta} _ {j} ^ {\top} ] L _ {j} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} [ \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} ] ^ {\top} \big) \big) ^ {1 / 2}\right) ^ {2}
$$

From Lemma 19 we conclude that  $\| L_j\|_{Q_\Delta, Q_{22}} \leq C_L^{\exp} \beta_j \gamma_j^{-1}$ , where  $C_L^{\exp} \coloneqq C_D(L_\infty) \varrho^{a_{22}}$ . This inequality and Jensen's inequality imply

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right) \leq \left(\mathrm {C} _ {L} ^ {\exp}\right) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} ^ {2} \left(\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \kappa_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}\right) \{\mathbb {E} [ \| \tilde {\theta} _ {j} \| ^ {2} ] \} ^ {1 / 2}\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq d _ {\theta} (\mathrm {C} _ {L} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} a _ {\Delta} ^ {- 1} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \kappa_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying (85) and Lemma 14 we get

$$
\mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} ]) \leq \mathrm {C} _ {2, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} + \mathrm {C} _ {2, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {2, 0} ^ {\exp} := d _ {\theta} a _ {\Delta} ^ {- 1} (\mathrm {C} _ {L} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} ^ {2} \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 2} / (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}),
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {2, 1} ^ {\exp} := d _ {\theta} a _ {\Delta} ^ {- 1} (\mathrm {C} _ {L} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} ^ {2} \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\exp} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}}
$$

To estimate the next term we rewrite it as follows

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)} = \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} N _ {\ell + 1, k} \delta_ {\ell} ^ {(2)},
$$

where

$$
N _ {\ell + 1, k} := \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)}.
$$

It is straightforward to check

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| N _ {\ell + 1, k} \| \leq \sqrt {p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta}} \| A _ {1 2} \| \kappa_ {\ell} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - (a _ {2 2} / 2) \gamma_ {s}) \tag {94} \\ \leq \mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp} \kappa_ {\ell} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}) ^ {- 1} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathrm{C}_N^{\exp} := (2 / a_{22})\sqrt{p_{22}p_\Delta}\|A_{12}\|(1 - a_\Delta\beta_\infty^{\exp})^{-1}$  and we used (compare with Lemma 12)

$$
\sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - \left(a _ {2 2} / 2\right) \gamma_ {s}) = \frac {1}{a _ {2 2}} \left\{1 - \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \left(1 - \left(a _ {2 2} / 2\right) \gamma_ {s}\right) \right\} \leq 2 / a _ {2 2}. \tag {95}
$$

Applying (96), Jensen's inequality and observation

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \delta_ {\ell} ^ {(2)} \| ^ {2} \right] \leq \mathrm {C} _ {2 2} ^ {\exp} \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \tilde {w} _ {\ell} \| ^ {2} \right], \tag {96}
$$

where  $\mathrm{C}_{22}^{\mathrm{exp}}\coloneqq \mathrm{C}_{\infty}^{2}\| A_{12}\|^{2}$  we obtain

$$
\mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}) ^ {\top} ]) \leq d _ {w} a _ {\Delta} ^ {- 1} \mathrm {C} _ {2 2} ^ {\exp} (\mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} \kappa_ {\ell} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}\right) \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {w}}.
$$

Applying (86) and Lemma 14 we get

$$
\left. \operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right) \leq \mathrm {C} _ {4, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {s = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {s}\right) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {4, 0} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \right. \tag {97}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {4, 0} ^ {\exp} := d _ {w} \mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {2 2} ^ {\exp} (\mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} (a _ {\Delta}) ^ {- 1} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 2} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}) ^ {- 1} \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {2},
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {4, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := d _ {w} \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \mathrm {C} _ {2 2} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} (\mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}) ^ {2} a _ {\Delta} ^ {- 1} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}}.
$$

To estimate the next term we proceed similarly. Using martingale property we obtain

$$
\mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)}) ^ {\top} ]) \leq d _ {\theta} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| A _ {1 2} \| ^ {2} (\mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} ^ {2} \kappa_ {\ell} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) ^ {2} (\beta_ {\ell} \gamma_ {\ell} ^ {- 1}) ^ {2} (1 + \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {w}})
$$

Hence, due to Corollary

$$
\operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right) \leq \mathrm {C} _ {5, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}\right) \mathrm {V} _ {0} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} + \mathrm {C} _ {5, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {5, 0} ^ {\exp} := d _ {\theta} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| A _ {1 2} \| ^ {2} (\mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} (\mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp}) / (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}),
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {5, 1} ^ {\exp} := d _ {\theta} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| A _ {1 2} \| ^ {2} (\mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp}) ^ {2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp})
$$

It follows from the previous inequalities that

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} (\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \} \{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \} ^ {\top}) \leq \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} + \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \right] \right|
$$

(98)

where

$$
C _ {2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} := 2 C _ {5, 0} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} + 2 C _ {2, 0} ^ {\exp},
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} := 2 \mathrm {C} _ {5, 1} ^ {\exp} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {2, 1} ^ {\exp}
$$

For the term  $\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Tr}(S_{k + 1}^{(3)}\{S_{k + 1}^{(2)} + S_{k + 1}^{(5)}\}^{\top})\right]$  we write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} \{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \} ^ {\top}) \right] \leq \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} \left[ \operatorname {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)}) ^ {\top} \right] \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} \left[ \operatorname {T r} (\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \} \{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \} ^ {\top} \right] \\ \leq \left\{\mathrm {C} _ {3, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} \beta_ {k + 1} + \mathrm {C} _ {3, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \gamma_ {k + 1} + p _ {\Delta} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} \operatorname {T r} (\Sigma) \beta_ {k + 1} \right\} ^ {1 / 2} \\ \times \left\{\mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} + \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1} ^ {2} \right\} ^ {1 / 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

We obtain

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} \left\{S _ {k + 1} ^ {(2)} + S _ {k + 1} ^ {(5)} \right\} ^ {\top}\right) \right] \leq C _ {3 / 2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}) V _ {0} + C _ {3 / 2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \tag {99}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} := \left(\mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {3, 0} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {3, 0} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp}\right) / 2,
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} := \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} / 2 + \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} / 2 + (\mathrm {C} _ {3, 1} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + p _ {\Delta} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} \operatorname {T r} (\Sigma)) \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} / 2 \\ + \left(\mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} \left(\mathrm {C} _ {3, 1} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + p _ {\Delta} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} \operatorname {T r} (\Sigma)\right)\right) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

Let us consider the term

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}\right) ^ {\top} \right] = \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \sum_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} \operatorname {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \tilde {w} _ {\ell} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell + 1, k} ^ {\top}\right), \tag {100}
$$

where  $Z_{j+1} \coloneqq V_{j+1} + A_{12}A_{22}^{-1}W_{j+1}$ . For  $\tilde{w}_{\ell}$  we can use the following expansion

$$
\tilde {w} _ {\ell} = \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {0: \ell - 1} ^ {(2)} \tilde {w} _ {0} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\ell - 1} \gamma_ {i} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {i + 1: \ell - 1} ^ {(2)} \widetilde {Z} _ {i + 1} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\ell - 1} \beta_ {i} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {i + 1: \ell - 1} ^ {(2)} C _ {i} A _ {1 2} \tilde {w} _ {i},
$$

where  $\widetilde{Z}_{i+1} := W_{i+1} + \kappa_i C_i V_{i+1}$  Substituting this expansion into r.h.s of (100) and repeating this procedure until  $\mathbb{E}[Z_{j+1} \tilde{w}_l^\top] = \gamma_j \mathbb{E}[Z_{j+1} \widetilde{Z}_{j+1}^\top (\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j+1: \ell_1-1}^{(2)})^\top]$  we come to the following expansion of (100)

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} \operatorname {T r} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \tilde {w} _ {\ell} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell + 1, k} ^ {\top}) \\ = \sum_ {\ell_ {1} = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell_ {1}} \sum_ {\ell_ {2} = j + 1} ^ {\ell_ {1} - 1} \beta_ {\ell_ {2}} \operatorname {T r} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \tilde {w} _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell_ {2}} ^ {\top} \left(\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell_ {2} + 1: \ell_ {1} - 1} ^ {(2)}\right) ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell_ {1}} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell_ {1} + 1, k} ^ {\top}\right) \\ + \gamma_ {j} \sum_ {\ell_ {1} = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell_ {1}} \operatorname {T r} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \widetilde {Z} _ {j + 1} ^ {\top} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: \ell_ {1} - 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell_ {1}} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell_ {1} + 1, k} ^ {\top}) \\ = \gamma_ {j} \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {k - j} \sum_ {\ell_ {1} = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell_ {1}} \sum_ {\ell_ {2} = 1} ^ {\ell_ {1} - 1} \beta_ {\ell_ {2}} \dots \sum_ {\ell_ {s} = j + 1} ^ {l _ {s - 1} - 1} \beta_ {\ell_ {s}} \\ \times \mathrm {T r} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \widetilde {Z} _ {j + 1} ^ {\top} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: \ell_ {s} - 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell_ {s}} ^ {\top} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell_ {s} + 1: \ell_ {s - 1} - 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell_ {s}} ^ {\top} \ldots (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell_ {2} + 1: \ell_ {1} - 1} ^ {(2)}) ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell_ {1}} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell_ {1} + 1, k} ^ {\top}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\ell_0\coloneqq k + 1$ . Using iteratively Corollary and estimate (94) for  $N_{\ell_1 + 1,k}$  we obtain the following bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \sum_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} \mathrm {T r} (\Gamma_ {j: k - 1} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \tilde {w} _ {\ell} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell + 1, k} ^ {\top}) \right\| \\ \leq \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}} \mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {j} \kappa_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\ell}) \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} [ \| Z _ {j + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} [ \| \widetilde {Z} _ {j + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {k - j} (\kappa_ {j} \| A _ {1 2} \| \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}}) ^ {s - 1} \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\kappa_{\infty}^{\exp} \leq (1/2)(\|A_{12}\| \mathrm{C}_{\infty} \varrho^{a_{22}})^{-1}$  and

$$
\mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} [ \| Z _ {j + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} [ \| \widetilde {Z} _ {j + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \leq 2 \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) (1 + \sqrt {\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}}) (1 + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {w}})
$$

we obtain that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \sum_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} \operatorname {T r} (\widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} Z _ {j + 1} \tilde {w} _ {\ell} ^ {\top} A _ {1 2} ^ {\top} C _ {\ell} ^ {\top} N _ {\ell + 1, k - 1} ^ {\top}) \right\| \\ \leq \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} \mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp} 2 \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) (1 + \sqrt {\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}}) \beta_ {j} \kappa_ {j} \prod_ {s = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) (1 + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\widetilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\widetilde {w}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

This inequality and (100) together imply

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {E} [ \mathrm {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}) ^ {\top} ] \right| \\ \leq \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} \mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\exp} 2 \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) (1 + \sqrt {\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}}) \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \kappa_ {j} \prod_ {s = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) (1 + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {j} ^ {\tilde {w}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, the standard arguments will lead to

$$
\left| \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} S _ {k + 1} ^ {(4)}\right) ^ {\top} \right] \right| \leq \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 4, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}) V _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 4, 0} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \tag {101}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 4, 0} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} \mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} 2 \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) (1 + \sqrt {\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}}) \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 2} (\mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}) / (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}) \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}},
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 4, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := \mathrm {C} _ {\infty} \mathrm {C} _ {N} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} 2 \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} (1 + \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) (1 + \sqrt {\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \mathrm {C} _ {\infty}}) \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}})
$$

Finally, we estimate all terms involving  $S_{k + 1}^{(6)}$ . We rewrite  $S_{k + 1}^{(6)}$  as follows

$$
S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)} = \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {l} M _ {\ell , k} W _ {\ell + 1}
$$

where we defined

$$
M _ {\ell , k} := \gamma_ {\ell} \beta_ {\ell} ^ {- 1} \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1}.
$$

Using martingale property we obtain

$$
\operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right) \leq \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} ^ {2} \| M _ {\ell , k} \| ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ]
$$

We rewrite  $M_{\ell ,k}$  as follows

$$
M _ {\ell , k} = \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \left[ \frac {\beta_ {j} \gamma_ {l}}{\beta_ {l} \gamma_ {j}} \mathrm {I} - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j} ^ {(1)} \right] \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} + \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \Bigg (\sum_ {j = l + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} - A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \Bigg)
$$

Since,

$$
\sum_ {j = l + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \Gamma_ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} = A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \bigg (\mathrm {I} - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(2)} \bigg)
$$

this equation leads to

$$
M _ {\ell , k} = \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \left[ \frac {\beta_ {j} \gamma_ {l}}{\beta_ {l} \gamma_ {j}} \mathrm {I} - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j} ^ {(1)} \right] \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {j + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j - 1} ^ {(2)} - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(1)} A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: k} ^ {(2)}
$$

We rewrite the term in the square brackets as follows

$$
\frac {\beta_ {j} \gamma_ {l}}{\beta_ {l} \gamma_ {j}} \mathrm {I} - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j} ^ {(1)} = \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \frac {\kappa_ {s}}{\kappa_ {s - 1}} \mathrm {I} - \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j} (\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {s} \Delta) = \sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \frac {\kappa_ {t - 1}}{\kappa_ {l}} \mathrm {I} \left\{\beta_ {t} \Delta + (\kappa_ {t} / \kappa_ {t - 1} - 1) \mathrm {I} \right\} \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {t + 1: j} ^ {(1)}
$$

Using assumption 2 we may show that

$$
\left| \kappa_ {t} / \kappa_ {t - 1} - 1 \right| \leq (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6) \beta_ {t}
$$

Taking norm of the both sides of the previous equation we obtain

$$
\left\| \frac {\beta_ {j} \gamma_ {l}}{\beta_ {l} \gamma_ {j}} I - \widetilde {\Gamma} _ {\ell + 1: j} ^ {(1)} \right\| \leq \sqrt {p _ {\Delta}} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) \kappa_ {l} ^ {- 1} \sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {j} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s})
$$

Finally we arrive at the following bound for  $M_{\ell ,k}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| M _ {\ell , k} \| \leq \sqrt {p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta}} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {s}) \tag {102} \\ + \sqrt {p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2}} \bigg (\| \Delta \| + \frac {a _ {\Delta}}{1 6} \bigg) \kappa_ {l} ^ {- 1} \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {s}) \\ \end{array}
$$

This bound will yield

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {T r} (\mathbb {E} [ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}) ^ {\top} ]) \leq 2 p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| ^ {2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {s}) \mathbb {E} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \\ + 2 p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) ^ {2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {\ell} ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \\ \times \left\{\sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - (a _ {2 2} / 2) \gamma_ {s}) \sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {j} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \right\} ^ {2} \\ =: A _ {1} + A _ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

The estimate of  $A_{1}$  follows from Lemma 14

$$
A _ {1} \leq C _ {6, 0, 2} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) V _ {0} + C _ {6, 1, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}. \tag {103}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {6, 0, 1} ^ {\exp} := 2 d _ {w} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}} (\mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp}) \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} / (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}),
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {6, 1, 1} ^ {\exp} := 2 d _ {w} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| ^ {2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}) \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}}
$$

Inequality (95) and Jensen's inequality together imply

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {2} \leq 2 (2 / a _ {2 2}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) ^ {2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {\ell} ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \\ \times \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \Bigg \{\sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {j} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \Bigg \} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - (a _ {2 2} / 2) \gamma_ {s}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Similarly, applying Jensen's inequality for the second time we come to the following inequality

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {2} \leq 2 (2 / a _ {2 2}) ^ {2} (1 / a _ {\Delta}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) ^ {2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {\ell} ^ {2} \mathbb {E} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \\ \times \sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {j} \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}\right) \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} \left(1 - \left(a _ {2 2} / 2\right) \gamma_ {s}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Changing the order of summation we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {2} \leq 2 (2 / a _ {2 2}) ^ {2} (1 / a _ {\Delta}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {s = j + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {j} (1 - (a _ {2 2} / 2) \gamma_ {s}) \\ \times \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {t - 1} \gamma_ {\ell} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {t - 1} \left(1 - \left(a _ {2 2} / 2\right) \gamma_ {s}\right) \mathbb {E} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, estimating  $\mathbb{E}\left[\| W_{\ell +1}\| ^2\right]$  by (85) and (86) and applying Lemma 14 we obtain

$$
A _ {2} \leq C _ {6, 0, 2} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) V _ {0} + C _ {6, 1, 2} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \tag {104}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {C} _ {6, 0, 2} ^ {\exp} := d _ {w} \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \left(\varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 4}\right) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 2} 2 \left(2 / a _ {2 2}\right) ^ {2} \left(1 / a _ {\Delta}\right) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2} \left(\| \Delta \| + \left(a _ {\Delta} / 1 6\right)\right) ^ {2} \\ \times \left(\mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp}\right) \left(\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}\right) ^ {2} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} / \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}\right) ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {6, 1, 2} ^ {\exp} := d _ {w} \tilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} (\varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2}) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}} 2 (2 / a _ {2 2}) ^ {2} (1 / a _ {\Delta}) ^ {2} p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) ^ {2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp})
$$

We conclude from (103) and (104) that

$$
\left. \operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right) \leq \mathrm {C} _ {6, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \left(1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}\right) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {6, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \right. \tag {105}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {6, 0} ^ {\exp} := \mathrm {C} _ {6, 0, 1} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {6, 0, 2} ^ {\exp},
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {6, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := \mathrm {C} _ {6, 1, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} + \mathrm {C} _ {6, 1, 2} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}
$$

It remains to consider  $\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Tr}(S_{k + 1}^{(3)}(S_{k + 1}^{(6)})^\top)\right]$ . We proceed similarly and use (102) to get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathrm {T r} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} (S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}) ^ {\top}) \right] \right| \leq \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} ^ {2} \| M _ {\ell , k} \| \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} [ \| Z _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \mathbb {E} ^ {1 / 2} [ \| W _ {\ell + 1} \| ^ {2} ] \\ = A _ {1} ^ {\prime} + A _ {2} ^ {\prime} \\ \end{array}
$$

The following estimate holds for  $A_1^\prime$

$$
\begin{array}{l} | A _ {1} ^ {\prime} | \leq \sqrt {p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta}} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| \max (d _ {\theta}, d _ {w}) (2 + 2 \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {1 / 2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {\ell} ^ {2} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \\ \times \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {s}) (1 + \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {w}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying standard arguments we get

$$
A _ {1} ^ {\prime} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0, 1} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \tag {106}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0, 1} ^ {\exp} := \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \sqrt {p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta}} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| \max  (d _ {\theta}, d _ {w}) (2 + 2 \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \times \left(\mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp}\right) \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} \kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} / \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1, 1} ^ {\exp} := 2 \widetilde {m} _ {V W} ^ {\exp} \sqrt {p _ {2 2} p _ {\Delta}} \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \| \max (d _ {\theta}, d _ {w}) (2 + 2 \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {1 / 2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\exp} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}) \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}}
$$

For  $A_2^\prime$  we write the following bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {2} ^ {\prime} \leq \sqrt {p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2}} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) \max (d _ {\theta}, d _ {w}) (2 + 2 \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {1 / 2} \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} \gamma_ {\ell} ^ {2} (1 + \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {\theta}} + \mathrm {M} _ {\ell} ^ {\tilde {w}}) \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {k} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \\ \times \left\{\sum_ {j = \ell + 1} ^ {k} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {s = \ell + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {s}) \sum_ {t = \ell + 1} ^ {j} \beta_ {t} \kappa_ {t - 1} \prod_ {s = t + 1} ^ {j} (1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {s}) \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

Changing the order of summation and applying arguments from the estimation of  $A_{2}$  we come to the following bound

$$
A _ {2} ^ {\prime} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0, 2} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1, 2} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \tag {107}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0, 2} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := \sqrt {p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2}} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) \max (d _ {\theta}, d _ {w}) (2 + 2 \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {1 / 2} \\ \times \left(\mathrm {C} _ {0, w} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {0, \theta} ^ {\exp}\right) \left(\varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 4}\right) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta} / 2} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} \left(\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}\right) ^ {2} / \left(1 - a _ {\Delta} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}\right) ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1, 2} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := \sqrt {p _ {\Delta} p _ {2 2}} (\| \Delta \| + (a _ {\Delta} / 1 6)) \max (d _ {\theta}, d _ {w}) (2 + 2 \| A _ {1 2} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} \|) ^ {1 / 2} (1 + \mathrm {C} _ {1, w} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \gamma_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {m t g}} + \mathrm {C} _ {1, \theta} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}) (\varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2}) ^ {2} \varrho^ {a _ {\Delta}}
$$

We conclude from (106) and (107)

$$
\left| \operatorname {T r} \left(\mathbb {E} \left[ S _ {k + 1} ^ {(3)} \left(S _ {k + 1} ^ {(6)}\right) ^ {\top} \right]\right)\right| \leq \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - \left(a _ {\Delta} / 4\right) \beta_ {\ell}) \mathrm {V} _ {0} + \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} \kappa_ {k + 1}, \left. \right. \tag {108}
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0} ^ {\exp} := \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0, 1} ^ {\exp} + \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0, 2} ^ {\exp},
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} := \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1, 1} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}} + \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1, 2} ^ {\mathrm {e x p}}
$$

Final estimate of the remainder term  $J_{k+1}$  Collecting (92), (93), (97), (98), (99), (101), (105), (108) we obtain the estimate of  $J_{k+1} \coloneqq R_{k+1} + J_{k+1}'$

$$
\boxed {| J _ {k + 1} | \leq C _ {0} ^ {\exp} \prod_ {\ell = 0} ^ {k} (1 - (a _ {\Delta} / 4) \beta_ {\ell}) V _ {0} + C _ {0} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {k + 1} (\gamma_ {k + 1} + \kappa_ {k + 1}),}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {C} _ {0} ^ {\exp} := \mathrm {C} _ {3, 0} ^ {\exp} \beta_ {\infty} ^ {\exp} + 3 \mathrm {C} _ {0 / 1} ^ {\exp} + 5 \mathrm {C} _ {4, 0} ^ {\exp} + 5 \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} \left(\kappa_ {\infty} ^ {\exp}\right) ^ {2} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 2 + 5, 0} ^ {\exp} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 4, 0} ^ {\exp} \\ + 5 \mathrm {C} _ {6, 0} ^ {\exp} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 0} ^ {\exp}, \tag {109} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {1} ^ {\exp} := \mathrm {C} _ {3, 1} ^ {\exp} + 5 \mathrm {C} _ {4, 1} ^ {\exp} + 5 \mathrm {C} _ {2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 2 + 5, 1} ^ {\exp} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 4, 1} ^ {\exp} + 5 \mathrm {C} _ {6, 1} ^ {\exp} + 2 \mathrm {C} _ {3 / 6, 1} ^ {\exp}
$$

Hence, we obtained (33).

# Appendix D. Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma 12 Let  $a > 0$  and  $(\gamma_k)_{k \geq 0}$  be a nonincreasing sequence such that  $\gamma_0 < 1/a$ . Then, for any integer  $k \geq 1$ ,

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) = \frac {1}{a} \left\{1 - \prod_ {l = 0} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \right\}
$$

Remark 13 If  $k_{0}$  is such that  $\sum_{l=0}^{k_{0}-1} \gamma_{l} \geq \log(2)/a$  then the r.h.s. of the previous equation is lower bounded by  $1/(2a)$  for any  $k \geq k_{0}$ .

Proof Let us denote  $u_{j:k-1} = \prod_{l=j}^{k-1}(1 - \gamma_l a)$ . Then, for  $j \in \{0, \dots, k-1\}$ ,  $u_{j+1:k-1} - u_{j:k-1} = a\gamma_j u_{j+1}$ . Hence,

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) = \frac {1}{a} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} (u _ {j + 1: k - 1} - u _ {j: k - 1}) = a ^ {- 1} (1 - u _ {0: k - 1}).
$$

Lemma 14 Assume A2 and set

$$
\varrho^ {a} = \frac {2}{a} \varsigma \max \{1, a _ {2 2} / (4 a _ {\Delta}) \} \vee \frac {4}{a} \varsigma^ {3}.
$$

The following holds

(i) For any  $a \in [a_{22}/4, \gamma_0^{-1}]$  and  $k \in \mathbb{N}$ , if in addition, we have  $\kappa \leq a_{22} / (4a_{\Delta})$ , then

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varrho^ {a} \gamma_ {k}, \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k}, \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k}
$$

(ii) For any  $a\in [a_{\Delta} / 8,\beta_0^{-1}]$  and  $k\in \mathbb{N}$

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varrho^ {a} \gamma_ {k}, \quad \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k}
$$

(iii) For any  $a \in [a_{\Delta} / 4, \beta_0^{-1}]$  and  $k \in \mathbb{N}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {3} / \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k} ^ {2} / \gamma_ {k}, \quad \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {4} / \gamma_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k} ^ {3} / \gamma_ {k} ^ {2}, \\ \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k} \gamma_ {k} \\ \end{array}
$$

(iv) For any  $a \in [a_{22}/4, \gamma_0^{-1}]$  and  $k \in \mathbb{N}$ ,

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k} / \gamma_ {k}, \quad \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k} ^ {2} / \gamma_ {k}, \quad \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {3} / \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varrho^ {a} \beta_ {k} ^ {3} / \gamma_ {k} ^ {2}
$$

Proof Part i) of the corollary, consider the first inequality and observe that

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) = \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \frac {\gamma_ {k - 1}}{\gamma_ {k}} \frac {\gamma_ {j}}{\gamma_ {k - 1}} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varsigma \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} \frac {\gamma_ {l - 1}}{\gamma_ {l}} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a)
$$

Note that as  $a \geq a_{22} / 4$ , we have

$$
\frac {\gamma_ {l - 1}}{\gamma_ {l}} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq (1 + \frac {a _ {2 2}}{8} \gamma_ {l}) (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq 1 - a \gamma_ {l} / 2
$$

Substituting into the above inequality yields

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varsigma \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a / 2) \leq \varsigma \frac {2}{a} \gamma_ {k}
$$

where we have applied Lemma 12 in the last inequality. Next and applying similar steps as before, we observe that

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varsigma \beta_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} \frac {\beta_ {l - 1}}{\beta_ {l}} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a)
$$

As we have

$$
\frac {\beta_ {l - 1}}{\beta_ {l}} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq 1 - \gamma_ {l} (a - \kappa a _ {\Delta} / 1 6) \leq 1 - \gamma_ {l} (a - a _ {2 2} / 6 4) \leq 1 - \gamma_ {l} a / 2
$$

we obtain

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varsigma \frac {2}{a} \beta_ {k}
$$

Similarly, using  $\beta_{j} \leq \kappa \gamma_{j} \leq a_{22} / (4a_{\Delta})\gamma_{j}$ , we obtain

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {l = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - \gamma_ {l} a) \leq \varsigma \frac {a _ {2 2}}{2 a a _ {\Delta}} \beta_ {k}
$$

For part ii) of the corollary, we observe that the first inequality can be proven by:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) = \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \frac {\gamma_ {k - 1}}{\gamma_ {k}} \frac {\gamma_ {j}}{\gamma_ {k - 1}} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \\ \leq \varsigma \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \frac {\gamma_ {j}}{\gamma_ {k - 1}} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varsigma \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} \frac {\gamma_ {\ell - 1}}{\gamma_ {\ell}} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Note that as  $a \geq a_{\Delta} / 8$ , we have

$$
\frac {\gamma_ {\ell - 1}}{\gamma_ {\ell}} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq (1 + \epsilon_ {\beta} \beta_ {\ell}) (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq 1 - a \beta_ {\ell} / 2
$$

Using Lemma 12, this yields

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \gamma_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varsigma \gamma_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell} / 2) \leq \frac {2}{a} \varsigma \gamma_ {k}
$$

Similarly, the second inequality is:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varsigma \beta_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \frac {\beta_ {j}}{\beta_ {k - 1}} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \\ \leq \varsigma \beta_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} \frac {\beta_ {\ell - 1}}{\beta_ {\ell}} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \varsigma \beta_ {k} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell} / 2) \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \frac {2}{a} \varsigma \beta_ {k} \\ \end{array}
$$

where (a) is due to the fact that we have  $\frac{\beta_{\ell - 1}}{\beta_{\ell}} (1 - a\beta_{\ell}) \leq 1 - a\beta_{\ell} / 2$ , and (b) is obtained by applying Lemma 12.

For the proof of part iii) we proceed similarly. We prove the second inequality only. The proof of the remaining follows the same lines. Denote  $\kappa_{\ell} := \beta_{\ell} / \gamma_{\ell}$ . Clearly,  $\kappa_{\ell - 1} / \kappa_{\ell} \leq \beta_{\ell - 1} / \beta_{\ell} \leq \varsigma$ . Then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} ^ {2} \kappa_ {j} ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \leq \varsigma^ {3} \beta_ {k} \kappa_ {k} ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \frac {\beta_ {j}}{\beta_ {k - 1}} \left(\frac {\kappa_ {j}}{\kappa_ {k - 1}}\right) ^ {2} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \\ \leq \varsigma^ {3} \beta_ {k} \kappa_ {k} ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} \left(\frac {\beta_ {\ell - 1}}{\beta_ {\ell}}\right) ^ {3} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell}) \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \varsigma^ {3} \beta_ {k} \kappa_ {k} ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \beta_ {\ell} / 2) \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \frac {4}{a} \varsigma^ {3} \beta_ {k} \kappa_ {k} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where (a) is due to the fact that we have  $\left(\frac{\beta_{\ell - 1}}{\beta_{\ell}}\right)^{3}(1 - a\beta_{\ell}) \leq 1 - a\beta_{\ell} / 4$ , and (b) is obtained by applying Lemma 12. Part iv) may be proved in the similar way.

Lemma 15 Let  $a > 0, p \geq 0, (\gamma_j)_{j \geq 0}, (\kappa_j)_{j \geq 0}$  and  $(u_j)_{j \geq 0}$  be nonnegative sequences. Then, for all integers  $k$ ,

$$
\sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k - 1} \kappa_ {j} \prod_ {\ell = j} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \gamma_ {\ell}) \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {j - 1} \gamma_ {i} ^ {p} \prod_ {n = i + 1} ^ {j - 1} (1 - a \gamma_ {n}) u _ {i} = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {k - 1} \gamma_ {i} ^ {p} u _ {i} \left(\sum_ {j = i + 1} ^ {k - 1} \kappa_ {j}\right) \prod_ {\ell = i + 1} ^ {k - 1} (1 - a \gamma_ {\ell})
$$

Lemma 16 (Lyapunov Lemma) A matrix  $A$  is Hurwitz if and only if for any positive symmetric matrix  $P = P^{\top} \succ 0$  there is  $Q = Q^{\top} \succ 0$  that satisfies the Lyapunov equation

$$
A ^ {\top} Q + Q A = - P.
$$

In addition,  $Q$  is unique.

Lemma 17 Assume that  $-A$  is a Hurwitz matrix. Let  $Q$  be the unique solution of the Lyapunov equation

$$
A ^ {\top} Q + Q A = I.
$$

Then, for any  $\zeta \in [0,\zeta_A]$ , where

$$
\zeta_ {A} := (1 / 2) \| A \| _ {Q} ^ {- 2} \| Q \| ^ {- 2},
$$

we get

$$
\| \mathrm {I} - \zeta A \| _ {Q} ^ {2} \leq (1 - a \zeta) \quad w i t h \quad a = (1 / 2) \| Q \| ^ {- 2}.
$$

If in addition  $\zeta \leq \| Q\| ^2$  then

$$
1 - a \zeta \geq 1 / 2.
$$

Proof For any  $x\in \mathbb{R}^d$  , we get

$$
\frac {x ^ {\top} (\mathrm {I} - \gamma A) ^ {\top} Q (\mathrm {I} - \gamma A) x}{x ^ {\top} Q x} = 1 - \gamma \frac {\| x \| ^ {2}}{x ^ {\top} Q x} + \gamma^ {2} \frac {x ^ {\top} A ^ {\top} Q A x}{x ^ {\top} Q x}
$$

Hence, we get that for all  $\gamma \in [0,(1 / 2)\| A\|_{Q}^{-2}\| Q\|^{-2}]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} 1 - \gamma \frac {\| x \| ^ {2}}{x ^ {\top} Q x} + \gamma^ {2} \frac {x ^ {\top} A ^ {\top} Q A x}{x ^ {\top} Q x} \leq 1 - \gamma \| Q \| ^ {- 2} + \gamma^ {2} \| A \| _ {Q} ^ {2} \\ \leq 1 - (1 / 2) \| Q \| ^ {- 2} \gamma . \\ \end{array}
$$

The proof follows.

Lemma 18 Assume that  $\| L\|_{Q_{\Delta},Q_{22}}\leq \varepsilon$  for some  $\varepsilon >0$  and

$$
0 \leq \beta \leq (1 / 2) \left\{\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + \varepsilon \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \right\} ^ {- 1} \tag {110}
$$

$$
0 \leq \gamma \leq (1 / 2) \| Q _ {2 2} \| _ {\mathrm {I}} ^ {- 2} \| A \| _ {Q _ {2 2}} ^ {- 2}. \tag {111}
$$

Set  $B_{11}(L) = \Delta - A_{12}L$ . Then, the equation

$$
L ^ {\prime} \left\{\mathrm {I} - \beta B _ {1 1} (L) \right\} = (\mathrm {I} - \gamma A _ {2 2}) L + \beta A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} B _ {1 1} (L) \tag {112}
$$

has a unique solution satisfying

$$
\| L ^ {\prime} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq (1 - \gamma a _ {2 2}) \| L \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} + \beta \mathrm {C} _ {D} (\varepsilon)
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {D} (\varepsilon) = 2 \{\| A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} + \varepsilon \} \{\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + \varepsilon \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \}.
$$

If  $\beta / \gamma \leq \varepsilon a_{22} / \mathrm{C}_D(\varepsilon)$ , then  $\| L' \|_{Q_{\Delta}, Q_{22}} \leq \varepsilon$ .

Proof Since  $\| L\|_{Q_{\Delta},Q_{22}}\leq \varepsilon$ , we get that  $\| B_{11}(L)\|_{Q_{\Delta}}\leq \| \Delta \|_{Q_{\Delta}} + \varepsilon \| A_{12}\|_{Q_{22},Q_{\Delta}}$ . Hence, using (110) and the triangular inequality, we get that  $\beta \| B_{11}(L)\|_{Q_{\Delta}}\leq 1 / 2$  and thus

$$
\left\| \mathrm {I} - \beta B _ {1 1} (L) \right\| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} \geq 1 / 2. \tag {113}
$$

Hence,  $\mathrm{I} - \beta B_{11}(L)$  is invertible and (112) has a unique solution given by

$$
\begin{array}{l} L ^ {\prime} = \left\{\left(\mathrm {I} - \gamma A _ {2 2}\right) L + \beta A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} B _ {1 1} (L) \right\} \left\{\mathrm {I} - \beta B _ {1 1} (L) \right\} ^ {- 1} \\ = \left(\mathrm {I} - \gamma A _ {2 2}\right) L + \beta D (L) \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
D (L) = \{A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} + (\mathrm {I} - \gamma A _ {2 2}) L \} B _ {1 1} (L) \{\mathrm {I} - \beta B _ {1 1} (L) \} ^ {- 1}.
$$

Using (113) and  $\| L\|_{Q_{\Delta},Q_{22}}\leq \varepsilon$ , we get that  $\| D(L)\|_{Q_{\Delta},Q_{22}}\leq \mathrm{C}_D(\varepsilon)$ . Hence, for  $\gamma$  satisfying (111), we get that

$$
\| L ^ {\prime} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq (1 - \gamma a _ {2 2}) \| L \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} + \beta \mathrm {C} _ {D} (\varepsilon) \leq \varepsilon + \gamma \bigl (\frac {\beta}{\gamma} \mathrm {C} _ {D} (\varepsilon) - a _ {2 2} \varepsilon \bigr) \leq \varepsilon ,
$$

where the last inequality is due to  $\frac{\beta}{\gamma} \leq \varepsilon a_{22} / \mathrm{C}_D(\varepsilon)$ .

Lemma 19 Let  $L_0 = 0$ . Assume that  $\| L_k\|_{Q_{\Delta},Q_{22}}\leq L_{\infty}$  and

$$
\begin{array}{l} 0 \leq \beta_ {0} \leq (1 / 2) \{\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \} ^ {- 1} \\ 0 \leq \gamma_ {0} \leq (1 / 2) \| Q _ {2 2} \| _ {\mathrm {I}} ^ {- 2} \| A \| _ {Q _ {2 2}} ^ {- 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

Then for any  $k\in \mathbb{N}$

$$
\| L _ {k} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {D} (L _ {\infty}) \varrho^ {a _ {2 2}} \beta_ {k} / \gamma_ {k},
$$

where

$$
\mathrm {C} _ {D} (L _ {\infty}) := 2 \{\| A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} + L _ {\infty} \} \{\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \}
$$

Proof Similarly to Lemma 18 we may show that

$$
L _ {k + 1} = (\mathrm {I} - \gamma A _ {2 2}) L _ {k} + \beta_ {k} D (L _ {k})
$$

where  $\| D(L_k)\|_{Q_\Delta ,Q_{22}}\leq \mathrm{C}_D(L_\infty)$ . Hence,

$$
\| L _ {k} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq \mathrm {C} _ {D} (L _ {\infty}) \sum_ {j = 0} ^ {k} \beta_ {j} \prod_ {s = j + 1} ^ {k} \left(1 - a _ {2 2} \gamma_ {s}\right)
$$

Application of Lemma 14 to the right hand side of the above completes the proof.

Lemma 20 Let  $L_{1} \coloneqq L_{0} \coloneqq 0$ . Assume that  $\| L_{k}\|_{Q_{\Delta},Q_{22}} \leq L_{\infty}$  and

$$
0 \leq \beta_ {0} \leq (1 / 2) \{\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \} ^ {- 1}
$$

$$
0 \leq \gamma_ {0} \leq (1 / 2) \| Q _ {2 2} \| _ {\mathrm {I}} ^ {- 2} \| A \| _ {Q _ {2 2}} ^ {- 2}
$$

$$
\beta_ {k - 1} - \beta_ {k} \leq \rho_ {\beta} \beta_ {k} ^ {2}, \gamma_ {k - 1} - \gamma_ {k} \leq \rho_ {\gamma} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2}
$$

$$
\beta_ {k} / \gamma_ {k} \leq (1 / (2 C _ {1} ^ {U})) a _ {2 2}
$$

with

$$
C _ {1} ^ {U} := 2 \left(\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + \| A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} + 2 L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}}\right).
$$

Then

$$
\left\| L _ {k + 1} - L _ {k} \right\| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq C _ {2} ^ {U} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2} \gamma_ {k + 1},
$$

where

$$
C _ {2} ^ {U} := 2 \rho_ {\gamma} L _ {\infty} \| A _ {2 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}} + 2 \rho_ {\beta} (L _ {\infty} + \| A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}}) (\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}})
$$

Proof Recall that  $B_{11}(L) = \Delta - A_{12}L$ . It follows from Lemma 18 that  $I - \beta_kB_{11}(L_k)$  is invertible matrix with bounded norm. Equation

$$
L _ {k} (\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k - 1} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k - 1})) = \left\{(\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {k - 1} A _ {2 2}) L _ {k - 1} + \beta_ {k - 1} A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k - 1}) \right\}
$$

may be rewritten as follows

$$
L _ {k} \left(\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} \left(L _ {k}\right)\right) = \left(\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {k} A _ {2 2}\right) L _ {k - 1} + \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} \left(L _ {k}\right) + E _ {k},
$$

where  $E_{k} := (\gamma_{k} - \gamma_{k-1})A_{22}L_{k-1} + (L_{k} + A_{22}^{-1}A_{21})D_{k}, D_{k} := -\beta_{k}A_{12}(L_{k} - L_{k-1}) + (\beta_{k} - \beta_{k-1})B_{11}(L_{k-1}).$  Let  $U_{k} = L_{k} - L_{k-1}$ . Then

$$
U _ {k + 1} \left(\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k})\right) = \left(\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {k} A _ {2 2}\right) U _ {k} - E _ {k}.
$$

Then

$$
U _ {k + 1} = (\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {k} A _ {2 2}) U _ {k} + \beta_ {k} (\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {k} A _ {2 2}) U _ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k}) (\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k})) ^ {- 1} - E _ {k} (\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k})) ^ {- 1}
$$

It is easy to check that

$$
\| (\mathrm {I} - \gamma_ {k} A _ {2 2}) U _ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k}) (\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k})) ^ {- 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq 2 \| U _ {k} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \{\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \}
$$

Moreover,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| E _ {k} \left(\mathrm {I} - \beta_ {k} B _ {1 1} (L _ {k})\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq 2 \rho_ {\gamma} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} L _ {\infty} \left\| A _ {2 2} \right\| _ {Q _ {2 2}} \\ + 2 (L _ {\infty} + \| A _ {2 2} ^ {- 1} A _ {2 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}}) \{\rho_ {\beta} \beta_ {k} ^ {2} (\| \Delta \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}} + L _ {\infty} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}}) + \beta_ {k} \| A _ {1 2} \| _ {Q _ {2 2}, Q _ {\Delta}} \| U _ {k} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \} \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying previous inequalities we obtain

$$
\| U _ {k + 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq (1 - \gamma_ {k} a _ {2 2} + C _ {1} ^ {U} \beta_ {k}) \| U _ {k} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} + C _ {2} ^ {U} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2}
$$

Since  $\beta_{k} / \gamma_{k}\leq (1 / (2C_{1}^{U}))a_{22}$  we obtain

$$
\| U _ {k + 1} \| _ {Q _ {\Delta}, Q _ {2 2}} \leq C _ {2} ^ {U} \varrho^ {a _ {2 2} / 2} \gamma_ {k + 1}
$$

Lemma 21 Let  $Q$  be a symmetric definite positive  $n \times n$  matrix and  $\Sigma$  be a  $n \times n$  matrix. Then

$$
\operatorname {T r} (Q \Sigma) \leq \| \Sigma \| _ {Q} \operatorname {T r} (Q).
$$

Proof Denote by  $(e_i)_{i=1}^n$  an orthonormal basis of eigenvectors of  $Q$ ,  $Qe_i = \lambda_i(Q)e_i$ ,  $i = 1, \ldots, n$ ,  $\langle e_i, e_j \rangle = \delta_{i,j}$ , where  $\delta_{i,j}$  is the Kronecker symbol. We get that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} (Q \Sigma) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle e _ {i}, Q \Sigma e _ {i} \rangle = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \langle e _ {i}, \Sigma e _ {i} \rangle_ {Q} \\ \leq \| \Sigma \| _ {Q} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \| e _ {i} \| _ {Q} ^ {2} = \| \Sigma \| _ {Q} \operatorname {T r} Q \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used  $\| e_i\| _Q = \lambda_i$  and  $\operatorname {Tr}Q = \sum_{i = 1}^{n}\lambda_{i}(Q)$

Corollary 22 If  $X$  is a  $n \times 1$  random vector such that  $\mathbb{E}[\|X\|_2] < \infty$ . Then,

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \| X \| _ {Q} ^ {2} \right] \leq \operatorname {T r} (Q) \| \mathbb {E} \left[ X X ^ {\top} \right] \| _ {Q}.
$$

Proof Note that  $\mathbb{E}[\| X\| _Q^2 ] = \mathrm{Tr}(Q\mathbb{E}[XX^\top])\leq \| \mathbb{E}[XX^\top ]\| _Q\mathrm{Tr}Q$

Lemma 23 Let  $m$  and  $n$  be two integers,  $P$  and  $Q$  be  $m \times m$  and  $n \times n$  symmetric positive definite matrices. Let  $X$  and  $Y$  be  $m \times 1$  and  $n \times 1$  random vectors such that  $\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^2] < \infty$  and  $\mathbb{E}[\|Y\|^2] < \infty$ . Then,

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ X Y ^ {\top} ] \| _ {Q, P} \leq \lambda_ {\min} (Q) ^ {- 1} \{\mathrm {T r} (Q) \} ^ {1 / 2} \{\mathrm {T r} (P) \} ^ {1 / 2} \| \mathbb {E} [ X X ^ {\top} ] \| _ {P} ^ {1 / 2} \| \mathbb {E} [ Y Y ^ {\top} ] \| _ {Q} ^ {1 / 2}
$$

Proof Note that  $\| \mathbb{E}[XY^{\top}]\|_{Q,P}\leq \mathbb{E}[\| XY^{\top}\|_{Q,P}]$  and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| X Y ^ {\top} \| _ {Q, P} = \sup _ {\| y \| _ {Q} = 1} \| X \langle Y, y \rangle_ {Q} \| = \| X \| _ {P} \sup _ {\| y \| _ {Q} = 1} \langle Q ^ {- 1} Y, y \rangle_ {Q} \\ = \| X \| _ {P} \| Q ^ {- 1} Y \| _ {Q} = \| X \| _ {P} \| Y \| _ {Q ^ {- 1}} \leq \lambda_ {\min } ^ {- 1} (Q) \| X \| _ {P} \| Y \| _ {Q}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ X Y ^ {\top} ] \| _ {Q, P} \leq \lambda_ {\min } ^ {- 1} (Q) \left\{\mathbb {E} [ \| X \| _ {P} ^ {2} ] \right\} ^ {1 / 2} \left\{\mathbb {E} [ \| Y \| _ {Q} ^ {2} ] \right\} ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

The proof follows from Corollary 22.

# Appendix E. Details on Numerical Experiments

This section provides details about the numerical experiments and verification that the convergence conditions are satisfied.

# E.1. Toy Example

In this toy example, we consider randomly generated instances of linear two timescale SA in the form (1), (2) with i.i.d. samples (and thus the martingale noise setting). In particular, we let the iterates  $\theta_{k}, w_{k} \in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  be  $d$ -dimensional and construct a problem instance as follows:

1. Sample a random matrix  $T$  whose entries are drawn i.i.d. from the uniform distribution  $U[-1, 1]$ ; Compute the QR-decomposition as  $T = QR$ .  
2. Set  $A_{12} = Q$  and  $A_{22} = Q^{\top}\Lambda_0Q$ , where  $\Lambda_0$  is a diagonal matrix with i.i.d. entries from  $U[-1,1]$ .  
3. Sample a random matrix  $R$  whose entries are drawn i.i.d. from the uniform distribution  $U[-1, 1]$ .  
4. Set  $A_{11} = RR^{\top} + I$  and  $A_{21} = Q^{\top}\Lambda_{1}$ , where  $\Lambda_{1}$  is a diagonal matrix with i.i.d. entries from  $U[-1,1]$ .  
5. Sample a stationary solution pair  $\theta^{*}, w^{*}$  with i.i.d. entries from  $U[-1,1]$ .  
6. Compute  $b_{1}, b_{2}$  using the generated matrices and stationary points, i.e.,

$$
b _ {1} = A _ {1 1} \theta^ {\star} + A _ {1 2} w ^ {\star}, \quad b _ {2} = A _ {2 1} \theta^ {\star} + A _ {2 2} w ^ {\star}.
$$

During the linear two timescale SA iteration, the noise terms are generated as

$$
V _ {k + 1} = F _ {V} ^ {k} + A _ {V, \theta} ^ {k} \theta_ {k} + A _ {V, w} ^ {k} w _ {k}, W _ {k + 1} = F _ {W} ^ {k} + A _ {W, \theta} ^ {k} \theta_ {k} + A _ {W, w} ^ {k} w _ {k}
$$

where  $F_V^k, A_{V,\theta}^k, A_{V,w}^k$  are vectors/matrices with entries drawn i.i.d. from the standard normal distribution  $\mathcal{N}(0,0.1)$ , and  $F_W^k, A_{W,\theta}^k, A_{W,w}^k$  are vectors/matrices with entries drawn i.i.d. from the standard normal distribution  $\mathcal{N}(0,0.5)$ . With the above constructions, it can be verified that the required assumptions A1, A3, A4 of the martingale noise setting hold. It remains to verify that the step sizes chosen satisfy A2.

Below, we show the plots of deviations in  $\theta_{k}$  and  $w_{k}$  without normalization by the step sizes (see Fig. 2).

![](images/3513352e9b28793254df8f9aeb815e38680140d253598dcae78e91ad1a569977.jpg)  
Figure 2: Unnormalized deviations from stationary point  $(\theta^{\star},w^{\star})$  and term  $I_{k}$  : the toy example.

![](images/96cdd1c3280707a5d46464098b8a7986d20f44a3cfcdf30478f83d5268bfc6bd.jpg)

# E.2. Garnet Problems

GTD Algorithm and Policy Evaluation Problem The specific form of linear two timescale SA used in this example follows from that of the classical GTD algorithm (Sutton et al., 2009a,b), which is described below for completeness. Let  $S, \mathcal{A}$  be some discrete state and action spaces (for clarity we bound ourselves by discrete setting, but one could formulate it in more general way),  $\gamma \in (0,1)$  and  $\pi : S \to \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})$  be a stochastic policy, i.e. mapping from states to probability measures over actions. When in state  $s$  the agent performs action  $a$  (distributed according to its policy  $\pi$ ), it transitions randomly to state  $s'$  with probability  $p(s'|s,a)$  and obtains reward  $r(s,a)$ . This induces a Markov chain with transition probabilities  $p_{\pi}(s'|s) := \sum_{a \in \mathcal{A}} \pi(a|s)p(s'|s,a)$ .
